Proposed algorithm:
1. Choose n = length of initial_h_values, maybe even 300. If n odd? Need sum n/2 exact integer; discretization requires sum(h)==n/2 exactly, so n must be even. initial n=300 even. We can use n.
2. Initialize binary sequence. Options:
   - Threshold initial h: h values >0.5 set 1, then adjust sum to n/2. But initial likely near binary? We can create by sorting values: ones for top n/2 (ties maybe). This binary projection may retain structure.
   - Random binary with seed.
   - Structured construction: e.g., take n/2 ones placed according to a low-discrepancy permutation. Maybe use greedy: start all zeros, add ones to minimize current max D. We can implement because n small.
3. Local search:
   - Maintain c = correlate(a,1-a,full) as int array length 2n-1. But for binary, c[0]=0. Objective C= max(c)*2/n.
   - Precompute for each position p the indices in c affected by flipping p. For a flip, delta vector can be computed as:
      For k=-(n-1)..n-1 excluding 0:
      idx = (n-1)+k.
      if p+k in [0,n-1]: if flip +1: delta += (1-a[p+k]); if flip -1: delta -= (1-a[p+k]) (but careful when a[p+k] changes if p+k is the other flipped? For swap, do sequentially and a changes. Fine.)
      if p-k in [0,n-1]: if flip +1: delta += -a[p-k]; if flip -1: delta += a[p-k].
      For k=0: binary c0=0 unchanged. But if applying continuous? For binary, ignore k=0.
   For swap (zero i ->1, one j ->0), total delta = flip_delta(i,+1 before j?) plus flip_delta(j,-1) maybe with intermediate states. We can apply both to c to evaluate, then rollback. Or derive combined O(n).

Compute c = correlate(h,1-h,full) can be done O(n log n) using FFT. Gradient for smooth max also can be computed faster if we have weights w over lags: For each h_i, gradient = sum over lags w_m * derivative of c_m. The derivative of c_m = sum over valid i: if k≥0: for i=0..n-1-k, derivative wrt h_i = (1 - h_{i+k}) - h_i? Wait c_m = ∑_{i=0}^{n-1-k} h_i (1 - h_{i+k}) for k≥0. Gradient wrt h_j: contributions where j as first: if j≤n-1-k: (1 - h_{j+k}); where j as second: if j-k≥0: -h_{j-k}. This matches. This can be computed by convolutions: For positive lags weights w_pos[k], grad_from_first[j] = ∑_{k≥1, j+k<n} w[k]*(1-h[j+k]) = (w_pos *? ) cross-correlation with h. Specifically first_contrib[j] = ∑_{k=1}^{n-1-j} w_pos[k] - ∑ w_pos[k] h[j+k]. This is convolution/correlation of w_pos with h and cumulative sums. Second_contrib[j] = -∑_{k=1}^{j} w_pos[k] h[j-k]. Similarly for negative lags if weights separate. We can also include k=0 term: c0=∑h_i(1-h_i), gradient 1-2h. If we include k=0 in max, yes previous did? np.correlate includes lag0 with h(1-h). For nonbinary h, c0 may be active. The previous grad loops included k≥0 and k<0; for k=0? They skipped? In grad_smooth_max loop m over 0..2n-2, if k>=0: grad[0:nv]+=(1-h[k:n]); grad[k:n]+=-h[0:nv]. For k=0, nv=n, grad += (1-h) - h =1-2h. Good. For FFT, we can include k=0 separately.

We can compute product convolutions:
For k≥0, product_sum_pos[k]=∑_{i=0}^{n-1-k} h_i h_{i+k}. This is autocorrelation of h at lag k (positive). Linear_sum_pos[k]=∑_{i=0}^{n-1-k} h_i. Then c_pos[k]=linear - product_pos[k].
For k<0, let r=-k>0: c_neg[r]=∑_{i=r}^{n-1} h_i h? q_i-r = h_i (1-h_{i-r}) = ∑_{i=r}^{n-1} h_i - ∑_{i=r}^{n-1} h_i h_{i-r}. Linear_neg[r]=∑_{i=r}^{n-1} h_i = total - prefix(r). Product_neg[r]=∑_{i=r}^{n-1} h_i h_{i-r} = autocorrelation of h at lag r (same as product_pos[r]!) Because product h_i h_{i-r} over i=r..n-1 equals product_pos[r] =∑_{j=0}^{n-1-r} h_j h_{j+r}. Indeed same set pairs. So product is symmetric. But linear differs: for negative lag r, overlap starts at r to n-1; positive lag r starts 0 to n-1-r. Thus c array:
for r=0: c0 = total_h - autocorr0 (autocorr0=∑h_i^2). (same)
for r>0:

This is easy. We only need autocorrelation A[r] for r=0..n-1 (nonnegative lags). A[0]=∑h^2. A[r] can be computed via FFT: If we take real FFT of h, power = |FFT(h)|^2, inverse gives circular autocorrelation? For linear non-circular autocorrelation, zero-pad to length ≥2n-1, FFT, multiply by conjugate, IFFT gives circular autocorrelation which for zero-padded length L≥2n-1 yields linear autocorrelation at lags 0..L-1. Specifically `acf = ifft(fft(h, L)*conj(fft(h,L))).real`; acf[0..n-1] = ∑_{i=0}^{n-1-r} h_i h_{i+r}? Let's verify: circular autocorr c[r]=∑_{i=0}^{L-1} h_i h_{(i+r) mod L}. With zero padding, for r<n, terms where i+r<n plus i+r≥L? none because L≥2n. Also terms i≥n zero. So c[r]=∑_{i=0}^{n-1-r}h_i h_{i+r}. Good. Also acf[L-r] corresponds negative lag but we only need nonneg. Great.

So c vector can be computed as:
prefix H cumulative length n+1. total=H[n]. A=linear_autocorr(h).
dx=2/n.
c = zeros(2n-1)
c[n-1] = (total - A[0])*dx
for r=1..n-1:
 c[n-1+r] = (H[n-r] - A[r]) * dx
 c[n-1-r] = (total - H[r] - A[r]) * dx
Check negative: For r=1, total-H[1]=sum h[1:], A[1]=∑_{i=0}^{n-2} h_i h_{i+1}; c_neg1=∑_{i=1}^{n-1} h_i(1-h_{i-1})=sum_{1..n-1}h_i - ∑_{i=1}^{n-1} h_i h_{i-1}; A[1]=∑_{j=0}^{n-2} h_j h_{j+1}; same terms j=i-1, yes ∑_{i=1}^{n-1}h_{i-1}h_i. Good.

This c computation is O(n log n) via FFT. Nice.

Gradient for weighted sum with separate weights pos_w[r] and neg_w[r], r=0..n-1 (r=0 shared). Let's derive using A? Weighted objective F=∑_{r=0}^{n-1} p[r] c_pos[r] + ∑_{r=0?} n[r] c_neg[r], where p[0]=n[0]= maybe half c0? Actually c0 appears once, but in our c vector only one c0. We can assign w0 weight to c0. For r>0, p[r] weight for positive lag, q[r] weight for negative lag.

F = ∑_{r=0} w0 c0 (if we split? Let p0+q0 = w0; set p0=w0, q0=0) + ∑_{r>0} p[r](H[n-r] - A[r]) + q[r](total - H[r] - A[r]).
Derivative wrt h_j.
Linear parts:
- From p[r] H[n-r] for r>0: H[n-r] includes h_j if j < n-r, i.e. r < n-j. Contribution p[r]. So linear_pos[j] = ∑_{r=1}^{n-1-j} p[r].
- From q[r](total-H[r]): total includes h_j always, -H[r] includes if j<r. So contribution q[r] (1 - 1_{j<r}). Thus linear_neg[j] = ∑_{r=1}^{n-1} q[r] - ∑_{r=j+1}^{n-1} q[r] = ∑_{r=1}^{j} q[r]. Yes S_neg prefix as before (for r≤j).
Product parts: -p[r] A[r] - q[r] A[r]. A[r]=∑_{i=0}^{n-1-r} h_i h_{i+r}. Derivative: for variable j, contribution to A[r] = h_{j+r} (if j+r≤n-1) + h_{j-r} (if j-r≥0), except if r=0 then A[0]=∑h_i^2 derivative 2h_j. Feasibility projection exact with box+sum:
```
def project_feasible(x,target):
 x=np.asarray(x,float).copy()
 # If already close sum, still clip.
 # We need solve f(τ)=sum(clip(x-τ,0,1))=target.
 # τ within [min(x)-1, max(x)+1]. Since target in [0,n].
 lo = x.min() - 1.0
 hi = x.max() + 1.0
 for _ in range(60):
   mid=(lo+hi)/2
   y=np.clip(x-mid,0,1)
   s=y.sum()
   if s > target: # need larger τ to decrease
       lo=mid
   else: hi=mid
 return np.clip(x-(lo+hi)/2,0,1)
```
This works if target between 0,n; f decreasing in τ. But if x far, lo/hi should bracket. f(max+1)=0; f(min-1)=n. Good. For target 0 or n, works. Complexity 60*n. For N=2048, 120k per projection; okay. L-BFGS uses no projection if penalty, only final. For Adam, project every step; 60*2048*1000=120M, could be heavy but okay? Maybe use fewer iterations 30. Optimization using L-BFGS with penalty:
- Define `lbfgs_phase(h,n,deadline,alphas,mus,maxiter)`.
No closures: We need top-level wrapper uses globals. We'll set globals.
```
G_N=0; G_ALPHA=1; G_MU=1; G_XNONE? no.
def _lbfgs_obj(x):
   c = compute_c_fft(x,G_N)
   mx = c.max()
   sh = G_ALPHA*(c-mx)
   # sum exp stable: max sh=0
   se = np.exp(sh)
   val = mx + np.log(se.sum())/G_ALPHA + G_MU*(x.sum()-G_N*0.5)**2
   return float(val)
def _lbfgs_grad(x):
   c=compute_c_fft(x,G_N)
   mx=c.max()
   sh=G_ALPHA*(c-mx)
   # set very negative to -inf? exp underflows ok
   w=np.exp(sh); w/=w.sum()
   p=np.empty(G_N); q=np.empty(G_N)
   p[0]=w[G_N-1]
   p[1:]=w[G_N:]
   q[0]=0
   q[1:]=w[:G_N-1]
   g = compute_grad_fft(x,G_N,p,q)
   g += 2*G_MU*(x.sum()-G_N*0.5)
   return g
```
Potential issue: if α large and c has many ties, w sum ok. If α=0? no.
`minimize` with `jac=_lbfgs_grad` will call grad; our grad recomputes c. Could cache but okay.

Simpler: top-level `STATE = {'n':0,...}`. Wrappers read. In run, `STATE['n']=n`. This is allowed. No closures.

L-BFGS phase details without closures:
```
def lbfgs_phase(h,n,deadline,alphas=(10,50,200,1000,5000,20000), mu_list=(1.0,10.0), maxiter=150, noise=0.0):
 h=project_feasible(h,n)
 best_c=compute_c_direct(h,n)
 bounds=[(0.0,1.0)] for n
 for alpha in alphas:
  for mu in mu_list:
    if time.time() > deadline - 5: return h,best_c
    STATE['n']=n; STATE['alpha']=alpha; STATE['mu']=mu
    try:
      res = minimize(lbfgs_obj, h, jac=lbfgs_grad, method='L-BFGS-B', bounds=bounds, options={'maxiter':maxiter,'ftol':1e-16,'gtol':1e-12,'maxfun':maxiter*20})
      h_new=project_feasible(res.x,n)
      c_new=compute_c_direct(h_new,n)
      if c_new<best_c:
         best_c=c_new; h=h_new
      # maybe if res fun lower direct? done
    except Exception: pass
    if time.time() > deadline -5: break
  if noise>0 and time left: perturb
 return h,best_c
```
But L-BFGS may be called many times; `bounds` list creation O(n). Fine.

def adam_phase(h,n,deadline,alpha_init=20,alpha_final=500000,lr_init=0.02,tol_stall=30):
 h=project_feasible
 best_c=direct
 if n large max_steps maybe 2000
 m=zeros; v=zeros; t=0; stall=0
 for step in range(max_steps):
   if time > deadline break
   c=compute_c_fft; mx=c.max()
   if mx<best: accept...
   alpha = alpha_init*(alpha_final/alpha_init)**(min(t/maxsteps,1))
   # maybe add random alpha occasionally
   sh=alpha*(c-mx); w=exp; normalize
   p/q; grad=compute_grad_fft
   # if grad norm tiny break?
   lr = lr_init*(1+0.01*t)**-0.75? or 1/sqrt
   Adam update
   h_new=project_feasible
   c_new=direct? use c_vec from compute_c_fft to avoid direct. We can have compute_c_fft returns c; max is exact up to numeric. Use direct for final but for step use c_new.max() okay. But direct and FFT may differ; use FFT for speed. For accepting best, update best_c_fft; final direct. 
   if c_new < best_c_fft - 1e-15: accept; h=h_new; stall=0 else: rollback; lr*=0.8; stall++
   if stall >100: add noise 0.05
```

Exact swap update implementation:
Precompute for binary array `a`, `c` int. Define function `apply_flip(c,a,p,s)` where s=+1 or -1? Could mutate c and a. For scoring many, we need rollback. We can implement `swap_update(a,c,i,j)` where i is zero to one, j one to zero:

To update c for a flip p with new value? Use old a before flip. For s=+1 (0->1) at p:
 for k=1..n-1-p: c[n-1+k] += 1 - a[p+k]
 for k=1..p: c[n-1-k] -= a[p-k]  # negative lag formula? Wait for positive lag index: c[n-1+k] contribution as first + (1-a[p+k]), as second? For flip 0->1, when p is second for positive lag k (i=p-k): term h_i(1-h_p) decreases by h_i. So for positive lag index k>0 where p-k>=0: c[n-1+k] += -a[p-k].
 For negative lag q>0 (q = -k): p as first for lag -q if p-q>=0: c[n-1-q] += 1 - a[p-q]; p as second for lag -q if p+q<n: c[n-1-q] += -a[p+q].
Actually the formula for all lags k:
- if p+k valid: c[index(k)] += s*(1 - a[p+k])
- if p-k valid: c[index(k)] += -s*a[p-k]
where s new addition (+1 for 0->1, -1 for 1->0), and k can be positive or negative. For s=+1. For k negative, p+k valid is left, adds 1-a[left]; p-k valid is right, subtracts a[right]. This is unified if loop over k from -(n-1) to n-1 excluding 0. We can vectorize with slices? For a single flip, loops over up to n. For exact candidate evaluation, O(n) with Python loops maybe too slow. We can update using numpy vectorized additions:
```
def apply_flip_array(c,a,p,s):
  # positive k 1..n-1-p
  if s==1:
    np.add.at? c[n:n+n-1-p] += 1 - a[p+1:n]
    if p>0: c[n:n+p] += -a[:p]
  elif s==-1:
    c[n:n+n-1-p] -= 1 - a[p+1:n]
    if p>0: c[n:n+p] += a[:p]
  # negative lags: index n-1-q for q=1..p (left) and q=1..n-1-p (right)
  # q where p-q valid: q=1..p
  if s==1:
    c[n-p:n] += 1 - a[:p]  # index n-1-q => for q=1..p, n-q = n-1..? Array slice c[n-p:n] corresponds indices n-p..n-1 for q=p..1? Need align.
```
Vectorizing with proper alignment tricky but doable.
Positive updates:
- p as first, k=1..n-1-p: index = n-1+k = n..n+n-1-p-1 length n-p-1? Actually n-1+1=n to n-1+(n-1-p)=2n-2-p. Value (1-a[p+1:n]). So `c[n:n+n-1-p] += s*(1-a[p+1:n])` length n-p-1. For s=-1 sign negative.
- p as second, k=1..p: index n..n+p-1. Value -s*a[:p]? For s=+1: -a[:p]. So `c[n:n+p] += -s*a[:p]`.
Negative q=1..p: index = n-1-q = n-1..n-p. We need c[n-p:n] (length p) corresponding q=p..1? For q=1 index n-1, value for s=+1: p-q valid left index p-1? Wait formula: k=-q. p+k = p-q, if valid (q≤p): term first p: s(1-a[p-q]). For q=1: left a[p-1], index n-1. q=p: left a[0], index n-p. So c[n-p:n] length p, values for q=p..1? If we take left segment a[:p] in order a[0]..a[p-1]. We need for index n-p (q=p) value 1-a[0]; index n-1 (q=1) value1-a[p-1]. So c[n-p:n] += s*(1 - a[:p][::-1])? Because segment a[:p] reversed maps to indices n-p..n-1: a[0] to n-p, a[p-1] to n-1. So use `a[:p][::-1]`.
- p as second for negative lag k=-q: p-k = p+q valid if q≤n-1-p. term -s*a[p+q]. Index n-1-q. q=1 index n-1, value a[p+1]; q=n-1-p index n-p, value a[n-1]. Need segment right a[p+1:n] length n-p-1. Index order n-p..n-1 corresponds q=n-1-p..1? For index n-p (q=n-1-p) value a[n-1]; index n-1 (q=1) value a[p+1]. So c[n:(n-p? wait indices n-p to n-1) length n-p-1? `c[n-p:n]` last? For n=10,p=3, q=1..6 indices 8,7,6,5,4,3. Segment a[4..9]. Need c[3:9] (indices3..8) values [a9,a8,...,a4]? c[3]=q6 value a9; c[8]=q1 value a4. So `c[n-p-1:n]`? n-p-1=6? Let's derive: number m=n-1-p. smallest index = n-1-m = p? Wait q max=m: index n-1-m = n-1-(n-1-p)=p. q=1 index n-1. So indices p..n-1 length m. For p=3,n=10, indices3..8. Yes. Segment a[p+1:n] reversed: [a9..a4] aligns indices3..8. So `c[p:n] += -s*a[p+1:n][::-1]` (length m). Similarly left segment p: indices n-p..n-1? For q=1 index n-1, q=p index n-p. left a[0:p] reversed aligns indices n-p..n-1. So `c[n-p:n] += s*(1-a[:p][::-1])`.
For s=-1, multiply by s: left `c[n-p:n] += s*(1-a[:p][::-1])` (since s=-1 gives negative), right `c[p:n] += s*(-a[p+1:n][::-1])? Wait formula second term -s*a[right]. For s=-1, -s*a = +a. Using `c[p:n] += -s*a[p+1:n][::-1]`. Good.
Positive: first `c[n:n+n-1-p] += s*(1-a[p+1:n])`; second `c[n:n+p] += -s*a[:p]`. Note these slices overlap? For positive indices n..; negative indices ≤n-1 except right negative includes p..n-1. STATE = {}

def compute_c_fft(h,n):
    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=float)
    L = 1 << (2*n - 1).bit_length()
    F = np.fft.rfft(h, L)
    acf_full = np.fft.irfft(F * np.conj(F), L)[:n]
    acf = acf_full.real
    # correct tiny: acf = np.clip(acf, 0, None) maybe
    pref = np.empty(n+1); pref[0]=0; np.cumsum(h, out=pref[1:]); pref = np.concatenate? Can't assign out if cumsum returns. Simpler:
    pref = np.concatenate(([0.0], np.cumsum(h)))
    total = pref[n]
    c = np.empty(2*n-1)
    c[n-1] = total - acf[0]
    if n > 1:
       idx = np.arange(1,n)
       A = acf[1:n]
       c[n-1+idx] = pref[n-idx] - A
       c[n-1-idx] = total - pref[idx] - A
    c = np.maximum(c,0.0) * (2.0/n)
    return c
```

`project_feasible(x,n)`:

x=np.asarray(x,dtype=float).copy()
target=0.5*n
lo=x.min()-1.0; hi=x.max()+1.0
for _ in range(50):
 mid=(lo+hi)*0.5
 s=np.clip(x-mid,0.0,1.0).sum()
 if s>target: lo=mid
 else: hi=mid
tau=(lo+hi)*0.5
y=np.clip(x-tau,0.0,1.0)
# adjust residual tiny
res=target-y.sum()
if res>0:
 idx=np.where(y<1.0-1e-12)[0]
 if idx.size: y[idx[0]] += min(res,1.0-y[idx[0]])
elif res<0:
 idx=np.where(y>1e-12)[0]
 if idx.size: y[idx[0]] += max(res,-y[idx[0]])
return y
```

`lbfgs_obj`/`grad`:
```
def lbfgs_obj(x):
    n=STATE['n']; alpha=STATE['alpha']; mu=STATE['mu']
    c=compute_c_fft(x,n); mx=c.max()
    sh=alpha*(c-mx)
    # if alpha large, sh all -inf? no.
    se=np.exp(sh)
    val=mx + math.log(se.sum())/alpha + mu*(x.sum()-0.5*n)**2
    return float(val)
```

def adam_phase(h,n,deadline,alpha_init=20.0,alpha_final=100000.0,lr=0.02,stall_max=80):
  h=project_feasible(h,n)
  best_c = compute_c_fft(h,n).max()
  m=np.zeros(n); v=np.zeros(n); t=0; stall=0; max_steps=5000
  for step in range(max_steps):
    if time.time()>deadline: break
    c=compute_c_fft(h,n); mx=c.max()
    if mx < best_c - 1e-15: best_c=mx; h_old=h.copy()?; stall=0 else if mx<best_c: best_c=mx
       if time.time() > deadline: break
       frac=min(t/1000,1)
       alpha=alpha_init*(alpha_final/alpha_init)**frac if alpha_final>alpha_init else alpha_init
       # occasionally random alpha? maybe if step % 100 ==0 use random from logspace
       sh=alpha*(c-mx); w=np.exp(sh); wsum=w.sum(); if wsum>0: w/=wsum
       p=np.zeros(n); q=np.zeros(n); p[0]=w[n-1]; p[1:]=w[n:]; q[1:]=w[:n-1]
       g=compute_grad_fft(h,n,p,q)
       # normalize if huge
       gn=np.linalg.norm(g)
       if gn>1e6: g/=gn
       t +=1
       mhat... beta
       step=lr*(1+t)**-0.5? Actually Adam uses lr. Use lr_cur = lr / (1.0 + 0.01*t)
       h_new=project_feasible(h - lr_cur*m_hat/(sqrt(v_hat)+1e-8),n)
       c_new=compute_c_fft(h_new,n); mx_new=c_new.max()
       if mx_new < best_c - 1e-15:
          h=h_new; best_c=mx_new; stall=0
       else:
          lr=max(lr*0.7,1e-6); stall +=1
          if stall > stall_max:
             # perturbation
             rng? Need random. Use np.random with global? Could use deterministic sinusoidal noise.
             h=project_feasible(h + 0.05*np.sin(np.arange(n)*1.7*randphase),n)
             stall=0
  return h,float(compute_c_direct(h,n))
```

`apply_flip` top-level:
```
def apply_flip(c,a,p,s):
 n=len(a)
 if p+1<n:
   c[n:n+n-1-p] += s*(1.0-a[p+1:n])
 if p>0:
   c[n:n+p] += -s*a[:p]
   c[n-p:n] += s*(1.0-a[:p][::-1])
 m=n-1-p
 if m>0:
   c[p:n-1] += -s*a[p+1:n][::-1]
 a[p] += s
```
Double-check right negative slice `c[p:n-1]` for p=n-1: m=0 skip. p=0: c[0:n-1] length n-1, segment a[1:n] reversed, indices 0..n-2. For p=0, negative lag q=1..n-1, p+q valid for all, term -s*a[q], index n-1-q from n-2 down to0. segment a[1:n] reversed: a[n-1] to a[1] aligns index0 (q=n-1) to n-2(q=1). Good.
Left negative p=0 skip. Positive p=0: c[n: n+n-1] += s*(1-a[1:n]) length n-1. Good.
Test n=2 flip p=0 s=+1: c pos: c[2:4? n+n-1-p=2+1=3 =>c[2:3] length1? Wait n=2, 2n-1=3 indices0,1,2. c[n:n+n-1-p]=c[2:3] yes length1: +1-a[1]. p>0 false. m=1: c[p:n-1]=c[0:1] += -1*a[1:2][::-1]= -a[1]. So c[2]+=1-a1 (positive lag1), c[0]+=-a1 (negative lag -1). If a=[0,0], c pos=1, c neg=0. Sequence 10: D_pos1=1 (1 at0 to zero1), D_neg1=0. Good. If then flip p=1 +1: before a=[1,0], c pos1=1,neg0. Apply p=1 s=+1: pos p+1<n false; p>0: c[2:3] += -a[:1] = -1 -> pos 0; c[1:2] += s*(1-a[:1][::-1])=1-a0=0. right m=0. c neg? c[1] before 0 remains. Sequence 11: c0? c[1] is lag0? `binary_local_search` details with rng:
```
def binary_local_search(a, deadline, target_count, rng):
 n=len(a); a=(a>0.5).astype(float); c=np.correlate(a,1-a,'full')
 best=float(c.max()); accept=0; last_recomp=0
 while time.time() < deadline - 1:
   if best <= target_count + 1e-12: break
   zeros=np.nonzero(a<0.5)[0]; ones=np.nonzero(a>0.5)[0]
   if zeros.size==0 or ones.size==0: break
   idx=int(np.argmax(c)); M=float(c[idx]); if M <= best: best=M
   if M <= target_count: break
   k=idx-(n-1)
   if k>0:
      v=np.arange(n-k); mask=(a[v]>0.5)&(a[v+k]<0.5); src=v[mask]
   elif k<0:
      q=-k; v=np.arange(q,n); mask=(a[v]>0.5)&(a[v-q]<0.5); src=v[mask]
   else:
      src=ones
   if src.size==0: src=ones
   # candidates: neighbor zero i+k if valid and zero, plus random zeros
   zero_list = zeros.copy()
   # shuffle small
   for _ in range(min(10, src.size)):
      i = int(src[_])
      neigh = i + k
      cands = []
      if 0 <= neigh < n and a[neigh] < 0.5: cands.append(neigh)
      # random 20 zeros
      ridx = rng.integers(0, zeros.size, size=min(30, zeros.size))
      cands.extend(int(zeros[rr]) for rr in ridx)
      # remove duplicates maybe set
      applied=False
      # remove source
      apply_flip(c, a, i, -1.0)
      baseM = float(c.max())
      bestM = best; best_j = -1
      # If even removing source alone improves? Need readd a one to keep sum. We need choose j.
      seen = set()

```
import numpy as np, time, math
from scipy.optimize import minimize

STATE = {}

def compute_c_fft(h, n):
    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=float)
    L = 1 << (2 * n - 1).bit_length()
    F = np.fft.rfft(h, L)
    acc = np.fft.irfft(F * np.conj(F), L)[:n].real
    pref = np.empty(n + 1)
    pref[0] = 0.0
    np.cumsum(h, out=pref[1:])
    total = pref[n]
    c = np.empty(2 * n - 1, dtype=float)
    c[n - 1] = total - acc[0]
    if n > 1:
        A = acc[1:n]
        c[n:] = pref[n - 1:0:-1] - A
        c[:n - 1] = total - pref[1:n] - A
    c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
    return c * (2.0 / n)
```
`np.cumsum(h, out=pref[1:])` works if h contiguous? yes. If h not contiguous, okay. `pref` length n+1.
For n=1: c length1, total=h[0], acc[0]=h0^2, c0=h0(1-h0); dx=2. But problem n large. OK.

`compute_c_direct`:
```
def compute_c_direct(h,n):
    return float(np.max(np.correlate(h, 1.0 - h, mode='full') * (2.0 / n)))
```

`project_feasible`:
```
def project_feasible(x, n):
    x = np.array(x, dtype=float, copy=True)
    target = 0.5 * n
    if x.sum() == target? still project? do.
    lo = float(x.min()) - 1.0
    hi = float(x.max()) + 1.0
    for _ in range(50):
       mid = 0.5*(lo+hi)
       s = np.clip(x - mid, 0.0, 1.0).sum()
       if s > target: lo = mid
       else: hi = mid
    y = np.clip(x - 0.5*(lo+hi), 0.0, 1.0)
    res = target - y.sum()
    if abs(res) > 1e-12:
       if res > 0:
          idx = np.where(y < 1.0 - 1e-12)[0]
          if idx.size:
             add = min(res, 1.0 - y[idx[0]])
             y[idx[0]] += add
       else:
          idx = np.where(y > 1e-12)[0]
          if idx.size:
             rem = max(res, -y[idx[0]])
             y[idx[0]] += rem
    return y
```
If res not fully adjusted due one idx insufficient, maybe need loop. But res tiny. Could loop over idx under 100. Use for robustness:
```
if res > 0:
 for k in np.where(y<1-1e-12)[0]:
   add = min(res, 1-y[k]); y[k]+=add; res-=add; if abs(res)<1e-12: break
else similar
```
But code length. Tiny okay.

`lbfgs_obj/grad`:
```
def lbfgs_obj(x):
    n=STATE['n']; alpha=STATE['alpha']; mu=STATE['mu']
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(x)): return 1e100
    c=compute_c_fft(x,n); mx=float(c.max())
    sh=alpha*(c-mx)
    se=np.exp(sh)
    ssum=se.sum()
    if ssum <=0: res=mx
    else: res=mx + math.log(ssum)/alpha
    err = float(x.sum() - 0.5*n)
    return float(res + mu*err*err)
```
If alpha very large, `alpha*(c-mx)` may be `-inf` for entries with c-mx=-inf? no. If c has nan? check.
`lbfgs_grad`:
```
def lbfgs_grad(x):
 n=STATE['n']; alpha=STATE['alpha']; mu=STATE['mu']
 c=compute_c_fft(x,n); mx=float(c.max())
 sh=alpha*(c-mx)
 w=np.exp(sh); s=w.sum(); 
 if s>0: w/=s
 else:
    w=np.zeros(2*n-1); w[int(np.argmax(c))]=1.0
 p=np.zeros(n); q=np.zeros(n)
 p[0]=w[n-1]; p[1:]=w[n:]; q[1:]=w[:n-1]
 g=compute_grad_fft(x,n,p,q)
 err=float(x.sum()-0.5*n)
 g += 2.0*mu*err
 return g
```
If alpha huge and c-mx has small negative, exp underflows; s active count. Good. If all underflow? impossible max exp0. If alpha=inf? no.

`lbfgs_phase`:
```
def lbfgs_phase(h, n, deadline, rng=None, noise=0.0):
    h = project_feasible(h, n)
    best_h = h.copy(); best_c = compute_c_direct(h, n)
    if n <= 600: alphas=(10.0,50.0,200.0,1000.0,5000.0,20000.0)
    elif n <= 1100: alphas=(20.0,200.0,2000.0,20000.0)
    else: alphas=(100.0,1000.0,10000.0)
    mus=(1.0,10.0)
    maxit = 300 if n<=400 else 150 if n<=900 else 80
    bounds = [(0.0,1.0)]*n
    for idxa, alpha in enumerate(alphas):
       if time.time() > deadline - 5: break
       for mu in mus:
          if time.time() > deadline - 5: break
          STATE['n']=n; STATE['alpha']=float(alpha); STATE['mu']=float(mu)
          x0=np.clip(h,0,1)
          try:
             res = minimize(lbfgs_obj, x0, jac=lbfgs_grad, method='L-BFGS-B', bounds=bounds, options={'maxiter':maxit,'ftol':1e-15,'gtol':1e-10,'maxls':40,'maxfun':maxit*20})
             h_try=project_feasible(res.x,n)
             c_try=compute_c_direct(h_try,n)
             if c_try < best_c - 1e-12:
                best_c=c_try; best_h=h_try.copy(); h=h_try
          except Exception:
             pass
       if noise > 0 and rng is not None and idxa < len(alphas)-1 and time.time() < deadline-20:
          # If h not improved? always explore a bit
          h_exp = project_feasible(h + noise * rng.standard_normal(n), n)
          # quick evaluate
          c_exp = compute_c_direct(h_exp,n)
          if c_exp < best_c + 1e-4:
              h = h_exp
    return best_h, best_c
```

If h_exp modestly worse, we don't use next alpha because we only set h if c_exp < best+1e-4 but if not, no. We may set h to worse to escape? Maybe help. We can set `if time.time()... and not improved: h=project_feasible(h + noise*rng.normal)` regardless. But could degrade and waste.       best_c=mx_new; best_h=h_new.copy(); h=h_new; stall=0
   else:
      lr = max(lr*0.7, 1e-6); stall +=1
      if stall > 80:
         # deterministic perturbation no rng: sine with random phase? Use time-based? We can pass rng maybe.
```

```
def greedy_binary(n, deadline, rng):
 a=np.zeros(n,float); c=np.zeros(2*n-1,float); m=n//2; added=0
 for _ in range(m):
   zeros=np.nonzero(a<0.5)[0]
   if zeros.size==0: break
   best_score=np.inf; best_p=int(zeros[0])
   for pi,p in enumerate(zeros):
      apply_flip(c,a,p,1.0)
      score=float(c.max()) + 1e-9*float(rng.random())
      apply_flip(c,a,p,-1.0)
      if score < best_score:
         best_score=score; best_p=int(p)
      if time.time() > deadline: break
   if best_p < 0: break
   apply_flip(c,a,best_p,1.0); added +=1
   a=(a>0.5).astype(float)
   c=np.correlate(a,1.0-a,mode='full')
   if time.time() > deadline:
      break
 # fill to m
 need=m-int(a.sum())
 if need>0:
   zeros=np.nonzero(a<0.5)[0]
   for p in zeros[:need]: apply_flip(c,a,float(p),1.0)
   a=(a>0.5).astype(float)
 return a
```

We'll check time before apply too. But if budget tight, run should return; we can if time > deadline: fill and return. Simpler.

`interpolate_h`:

def interpolate_h(h_old, n_old, n_new):
 if n_new == len(h_old): return np.array(h_old)
 x_old=np.linspace(0.0,2.0,n_old,endpoint=False)
 x_new=np.linspace(0.0,2.0,n_new,endpoint=False)

```
def binary_local_search(a, deadline, target_count, rng):
    n=len(a); a=(a>0.5).astype(float)
    c=np.correlate(a,1-a,mode='full')
    best=float(c.max()); accept=0
    while time.time() < deadline - 1:
        if best <= target_count: break
        zeros=np.nonzero(a<0.5)[0]; ones=np.nonzero(a>0.5)[0]
        if not zeros.size or not ones.size: break
        idx=int(np.argmax(c)); M=float(c[idx])
        if M < best: best=M
        if M <= target_count: break
        k=idx-(n-1)
        if k>0:
            v=np.arange(n-k); src=v[(a[v]>0.5)&(a[v+k]<0.5)]
        elif k<0:
            q=-k; v=np.arange(q,n); src=v[(a[v]>0.5)&(a[v-q]<0.5)]
        else: src=ones
        if not src.size: src=ones
        for ti in range(min(8, src.size)):
            i=int(src[ti]); neigh=i+k
            cands=[]
            if 0 <= neigh < n and a[neigh] < 0.5: cands.append(int(neigh))
            if zeros.size:
                rs=rng.integers(0, zeros.size, size=min(24, zeros.size))
                for rr in rs: cands.append(int(zeros[int(rr)]))
            apply_flip(c,a,i,-1.0)
            bestM=best; best_j=-1
            seen=set()
            for j in cands:
                if j in seen or j==i or a[j]>0.5: continue
                seen.add(j)
                apply_flip(c,a,j,1.0)
                m=float(c.max())
                apply_flip(c,a,j,-1.0)
                if m < bestM - 1e-9:
                    bestM=m; best_j=j
            if best_j >= 0 and bestM < best - 1e-9:
                apply_flip(c,a,best_j,1.0)
                best=bestM; accept += 1
                if accept % 40 == 0:
                    a=(a>0.5).astype(float); c=np.correlate(a,1-a,mode='full'); best=float(c.max())
            else:
                apply_flip(c,a,i,1.0)
    return a
```
If remove source i and best_j found, we apply +best_j outside; but note inside inner loop for best_j, we had undone that j after measuring. So state now i=0, all others original. Applying +best_j yields final. c currently state i=0; applying +best_j updates to final. Good.
If no best, we apply +i to original. Good.

```python
import numpy as np
import time
import math
from scipy.optimize import minimize

STATE = {}

def compute_c_fft(h, n):
    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=float)
    L = 1 << (2 * n - 1).bit_length()
    F = np.fft.rfft(h, L)
    acc = np.fft.irfft(F * np.conj(F), L)[:n].real
    pref = np.empty(n + 1)
    pref[0] = 0.0
    np.cumsum(h, out=pref[1:])
    total = pref[n]
    c = np.empty(2 * n - 1, dtype=float)
    c[n - 1] = total - acc[0]
    if n > 1:
        A = acc[1:n]
        c[n:] = pref[n - 1:0:-1] - A
        c[:n - 1] = total - pref[1:n] - A
    return np.maximum(c, 0.0) * (2.0 / n)

def compute_c_direct(h, n):
    return float(np.max(np.correlate(h, 1.0 - h, mode='full') * (2.0 / n)))

def project_feasible(x, n):
    x = np.array(x, dtype=float, copy=True)
    target = 0.5 * n
    lo = float(x.min()) - 1.0
    hi = float(x.max()) + 1.0
    for _ in range(50):
        mid = 0.5 * (lo + hi)
        s = np.clip(x - mid, 0.0, 1.0).sum()
        if s > target:
            lo = mid
        else:
            hi = mid
    y = np.clip(x - 0.5 * (lo + hi), 0.0, 1.0)
    res = target - y.sum()
    if abs(res) > 1e-12:
        if res > 0:
            idx = np.where(y < 1.0 - 1e-12)[0]
            for k in idx:
                add = min(res, 1.0 - y[k])
                y[k] += add
                res -= add
                if abs(res) < 1e-12:
                    break
        else:
            idx = np.where(y > 1e-12)[0]
            for k in idx:
                rem = max(res, -y[k])
                y[k] += rem
                res -= rem
                if abs(res) < 1e-12:
                    break
    return y

def compute_grad_fft(h, n, p, q):
    w = p + q
    w0 = float(w[0])
    w = w.copy()
    w[0] = 0.0
    grad = w0 * (1.0 - 2.0 * h)
    if n > 1:
        qpref = np.zeros(n)
        qpref[1:] = np.cumsum(q[1:])
        ppref = np.empty(n)
        ppref[0] = 0.0
        np.cumsum(p[1:], out=ppref[1:])
        psuf = np.zeros(n)
        psuf[:-1] = ppref[n - 1:0:-1]
        grad += psuf + qpref
    L = 1 << (2 * n - 1).bit_length()
    fw = np.fft.rfft(w, L)
    fh = np.fft.rfft(h, L)
    conv1 = np.fft.irfft(fw * fh, L)[:n]
    fr = np.fft.rfft(h[::-1], L)
    conv2 = np.fft.irfft(fw * fr, L)[:n]
    grad -= conv2[::-1] + conv1
    return grad * (2.0 / n)

def lbfgs_obj(x):
    n = STATE['n']
    alpha = STATE['alpha']
    mu = STATE['mu']
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(x)):
        return 1e100
    c = compute_c_fft(x, n)
    mx = float(c.max())
    sh = alpha * (c - mx)
    se = np.exp(sh)
    ssum = float(se.sum())
    val = mx + (math.log(ssum) / alpha if ssum > 0.0 else 0.0)
    err = float(x.sum() - 0.5 * n)
    return float(val + mu * err * err)

def lbfgs_grad(x):
    n = STATE['n']
    alpha = STATE['alpha']
    mu = STATE['mu']
    c = compute_c_fft(x, n)
    mx = float(c.max())
    sh = alpha * (c - mx)
    w = np.exp(sh)
    s = float(w.sum())
    if s <= 0.0:
        w = np.zeros(2 * n - 1)
        w[int(np.argmax(c))] = 1.0
    else:
        w /= s
    p = np.zeros(n)
    q = np.zeros(n)
    p[0] = w[n - 1]
    p[1:] = w[n:]
    q[1:] = w[:n - 1]
    g = compute_grad_fft(x, n, p, q)
    err = float(x.sum() - 0.5 * n)
    g += 2.0 * mu * err
    return g

def lbfgs_phase(h, n, deadline, rng=None, noise=0.0):
    h = project_feasible(h, n)
    best_h = h.copy()
    best_c = float(compute_c_fft(h, n).max())
    if n <= 600:
        alphas = (10.0, 50.0, 200.0, 1000.0, 5000.0, 20000.0)
    elif n <= 1100:
        alphas = (20.0, 200.0, 2000.0, 20000.0, 80000.0)
    else:
        alphas = (60.0, 600.0, 6000.0, 50000.0)
    mus = (1.0, 10.0)
    maxit = 300 if n <= 400 else (150 if n <= 900 else 80)
    bounds = [(0.0, 1.0)] * n
    for ia, alpha in enumerate(alphas):
        if time.time() > deadline - 5:
            break
        for mu in mus:
            if time.time() > deadline - 5:
                break
            STATE['n'] = n
            STATE['alpha'] = float(alpha)
            STATE['mu'] = float(mu)
            try:
                res = minimize(lbfgs_obj, np.clip(h, 0.0, 1.0), jac=lbfgs_grad,
                               method='L-BFGS-B', bounds=bounds,
                               options={'maxiter': maxit, 'ftol': 1e-15,
                                        'gtol': 1e-10, 'maxls': 40,
                                        'maxfun': maxit * 20})
                hx = project_feasible(res.x, n)
                cx = float(compute_c_fft(hx, n).max())
                if cx < best_c - 1e-13:
                    best_c = cx
                    best_h = hx.copy()
                    h = hx
                elif cx < best_c + 0.01:
                    h = hx
            except Exception:
                pass
        if noise > 0.0 and rng is not None and ia < len(alphas) - 2 and time.time() < deadline - 20:
            try:
                hx = project_feasible(h + noise * rng.standard_normal(n), n)
                if float(compute_c_fft(hx, n).max()) < best_c + 0.03:
                    h = hx
            except Exception:
                pass
    return best_h, best_c

def adam_phase(h, n, deadline, rng=None):
    h = project_feasible(h, n)
    best_h = h.copy()
    best_c = float(compute_c_fft(h, n).max())
    m = np.zeros(n)
    v = np.zeros(n)
    t = 0
    stall = 0
    lr = 0.06
    p = np.zeros(n)
    q = np.zeros(n)
    max_steps = 20000
    for step in range(max_steps):
        if time.time() > deadline - 2:
            break
        c = compute_c_fft(h, n)
        mx = float(c.max())
        if mx < best_c - 1e-14:
            best_c = mx
            best_h = h.copy()
        if time.time() > deadline - 2:
            break
        frac = min(1.0, 0.2 + 0.8 * step / 800.0)
        alpha = 10.0 * (200000.0 / 10.0) ** frac
        sh = alpha * (c - mx)
        w = np.exp(sh)
        ws = float(w.sum())
        if ws <= 0.0:
            w = np.zeros(2 * n - 1)
            w[int(np.argmax(c))] = 1.0
        else:
            w /= ws
        p.fill(0.0)
        q.fill(0.0)
        p[0] = w[n - 1]
        p[1:] = w[n:]
        q[1:] = w[:n - 1]
        g = compute_grad_fft(h, n, p, q)
        t += 1
        m = 0.9 * m + 0.1 * g
        v = 0.999 * v + 0.001 * (g * g)
        mh = m / (1.0 - 0.9 ** t)
        vh = v / (1.0 - 0.999 ** t)
        hc = lr / (1.0 + 0.02 * t)
        h_new = project_feasible(h - hc * mh / (np.sqrt(vh) + 1e-8), n)
        c_new = compute_c_fft(h_new, n)
        mx_new = float(c_new.max())
        if mx_new < best_c - 1e-14:
            best_c = mx_new
            best_h = h_new.copy()
            h = h_new
            stall = 0
        else:
            lr = max(lr * 0.7, 1e-6)
            stall += 1
            if stall > 100 and rng is not None:
                h = project_feasible(h + 0.045 * rng.standard_normal(n), n)
                m = np.zeros(n)
                v = np.zeros(n)
                stall = 0
    return best_h, best_c

def apply_flip(c, a, p, s):
    n = len(a)
    if p + 1 < n:
        c[n:n + n - 1 - p] += s * (1.0 - a[p + 1:n])
    if p > 0:
        c[n:n + p] += -s * a[:p]
        c[n - p:n] += s * (1.0 - a[:p][::-1])
    if n - 1 - p > 0:
        c[p:n - 1] += -s * a[p + 1:n][::-1]
    a[p] += s

def greedy_binary(n, deadline, rng):
    a = np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
    c = np.zeros(2 * n - 1, dtype=float)
    m = n // 2
    for _ in range(m):
        if time.time() > deadline - 5:
            break
        zeros = np.nonzero(a < 0.5)[0]
        if not zeros.size:
            break
        best_score = np.inf
        best_p = int(zeros[0])
        for ii in range(zeros.size):
            p = int(zeros[ii])
            apply_flip(c, a, p, 1.0)
            score = float(c.max()) + 1e-9 * float(rng.random())
            apply_flip(c, a, p, -1.0)
            if score < best_score:
                best_score = score
                best_p = p
            if time.time() > deadline - 8:
                break
        apply_flip(c, a, best_p, 1.0)
        a = (a > 0.5).astype(float)
        c = np.correlate(a, 1.0 - a, mode='full')
    need = m - int(a.sum())
    if need > 0:
        zeros = np.nonzero(a < 0.5)[0]
        for p in zeros[:need]:
            apply_flip(c, a, int(p), 1.0)
        a = (a > 0.5).astype(float)
    return a

def binary_local_search(a, deadline, target_count, rng):
    n = len(a)
    a = (a > 0.5).astype(float)
    c = np.correlate(a, 1.0 - a, mode='full')
    best = float(c.max())
    accept = 0
    while time.time() < deadline - 1:
        if best <= float(target_count):
            break
        zeros = np.nonzero(a < 0.5)[0]
        ones = np.nonzero(a > 0.5)[0]
        if not zeros.size or not ones.size:
            break
        idx = int(np.argmax(c))
        M = float(c[idx])
        if M < best:
            best = M
        if M <= float(target_count):
            break
        k = idx - (n - 1)
        if k > 0:
            v = np.arange(n - k)
            src = v[(a[v] > 0.5) & (a[v + k] < 0.5)]
        elif k < 0:
            q = -k
            v = np.arange(q, n)
            src = v[(a[v] > 0.5) & (a[v - q] < 0.5)]
        else:
            src = ones
        if not src.size:
            src = ones
        for ti in range(min(8, src.size)):
            i = int(src[ti])
            if a[i] < 0.5:
                continue
            neigh = i + k
            cands = []
            if 0 <= neigh < n and a[neigh] < 0.5:
                cands.append(int(neigh))
            if zeros.size:
                rs = rng.integers(0, zeros.size, size=min(24, zeros.size))
                for rr in rs:
                    cands.append(int(zeros[int(rr)]))
            apply_flip(c, a, i, -1.0)
            bestM = best
            best_j = -1
            seen = set()
            for j in cands:
                if j in seen or j == i or a[j] > 0.5:
                    continue
                seen.add(j)
                apply_flip(c, a, j, 1.0)
                mm = float(c.max())
                apply_flip(c, a, j, -1.0)
                if mm < bestM - 1e-9:
                    bestM = mm
                    best_j = j
            if best_j >= 0 and bestM < best - 1e-9:
                apply_flip(c, a, best_j, 1.0)
                best = bestM
                accept += 1
                if accept % 40 == 0:
                    a = (a > 0.5).astype(float)
                    c = np.correlate(a, 1.0 - a, mode='full')
                    best = float(c.max())
            else:
                apply_flip(c, a, i, 1.0)
    return a

def make_binary(h, n):
    b = np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
    t = n // 2
    if 0 < t < n:
        idx = np.argpartition(h, -t)[-t:]
        b[idx] = 1.0
    elif t >= n:
        b[:] = 1.0
    return project_feasible(b, n)

def interpolate_h(h_old, n_old, n_new):
    if n_new == n_old:
        return np.array(h_old, dtype=float, copy=True)
    x_old = np.linspace(0.0, 2.0, n_old, endpoint=False)
    x_new = np.linspace(0.0, 2.0, n_new, endpoint=False)
    h_new = np.interp(x_new, x_old, h_old, left=h_old[0], right=h_old[-1])
    return project_feasible(h_new, n_new)

def run(seed=42, budget_s=1100, **kwargs):
    t0 = time.time()
    deadline = t0 + max(20.0, float(budget_s) - 5.0)
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    try:
        h0 = np.array(initial_h_values, dtype=float).copy()
        n0 = int(h0.size)
    except Exception:
        n0 = 300
        h0 = np.full(n0, 0.5)
    if n0 % 2:
        h0 = h0[:n0 - 1]
        n0 -= 1
    if n0 < 4:
        n0 = 300
        h0 = np.full(n0, 0.5)
    h0 = project_feasible(h0, n0)
    best_h = h0.copy()
    best_c = compute_c_direct(best_h, n0)

    try:
        if n0 <= 600 and deadline - time.time() > 180:
            a = greedy_binary(n0, min(time.time() + 70, deadline - 150), rng)
            hb = project_feasible(a, n0)
            cb = compute_c_direct(hb, n0)
            if cb < best_c:
                best_c = cb
                best_h = hb.copy()
            cands = [a.copy(), make_binary(h0, n0), make_binary(best_h, n0)]
            best_bin = None
            best_bc = np.inf
            for bc in cands:
                ht = project_feasible(bc, n0)
                ct = compute_c_direct(ht, n0)
                if ct < best_bc:
                    best_bc = ct
                    best_bin = ht
            if best_bin is not None and deadline - time.time() > 35:
                tc = int(math.floor(0.3808 * n0 / 2.0))
                al = binary_local_search(best_bin, min(time.time() + 55, deadline - 40), tc, rng)
                hloc = project_feasible(al, n0)
                cloc = compute_c_direct(hloc, n0)
                if cloc < best_c:
                    best_c = cloc
                    best_h = hloc.copy()

        maxN = 1200
        Ns = []
        for x in (n0, 2 * n0, 4 * n0, 800, 1200):
            x = int(round(x))
            if x < 4:
                continue
            if x > maxN:
                x = maxN
            if x % 2:
                x -= 1 if x > n0 else 1
            if x >= n0 and x not in Ns:
                Ns.append(x)
        Ns.sort()

        if len(Ns) == 1:
            fracs = [1.0]
        elif len(Ns) == 2:
            fracs = [0.40, 0.60]
        elif len(Ns) == 3:
            fracs = [0.20, 0.35, 0.45]
        elif len(Ns) == 4:
            fracs = [0.15, 0.25, 0.30, 0.30]
        else:
            fracs = [0.12] * len(Ns)

        for ni, N in enumerate(Ns):
            rem = deadline - time.time()
            if rem < 80:
                break
            alloc = rem * fracs[ni]
            t_end = min(deadline - 30, time.time() + max(40.0, alloc))
            if N == len(best_h):
                starts = [best_h.copy()]
            elif N > len(best_h):
                hi = interpolate_h(best_h, len(best_h), N)
                starts = [hi, make_binary(hi, N)]
            else:
                continue
            for si, st in enumerate(starts):
                if time.time() > t_end - 35:
                    break
                h = project_feasible(st, N)
                c = compute_c_direct(h, N)
                if c < best_c:
                    best_c = c
                    best_h = h.copy()
                t_ph = min(t_end - 15, time.time() + max(25.0, (t_end - time.time()) * 0.55 / max(1, len(starts) - si)))
                ht, ct = lbfgs_phase(h, N, t_ph, rng, noise=0.012)
                cdirect = compute_c_direct(ht, N)
                if cdirect < best_c:
                    best_c = cdirect
                    best_h = ht.copy()
                if time.time() < t_end - 25:
                    ta = min(t_end - 10, time.time() + max(25.0, (t_end - time.time()) * 0.55 / max(1, len(starts) - si)))
                    ha, ca = adam_phase(ht, N, ta, rng)
                    cad = compute_c_direct(ha, N)
                    if cad < best_c:
                        best_c = cad
                        best_h = ha.copy()
                if N <= 600 and time.time() < t_end - 12:
                    tc = int(math.floor(0.3808 * N / 2.0))
                    ab = binary_local_search(ha, min(t_end - 5, time.time() + 18), tc, rng)
                    hbi = project_feasible(ab, N)
                    cbi = compute_c_direct(hbi, N)
                    if cbi < best_c:
                        best_c = cbi
                        best_h = hbi.copy()
        if deadline - time.time() > 18:
            nfin = len(best_h)
            hp, cp = lbfgs_phase(best_h, nfin, deadline - 2, rng, noise=0.0)
            cdir = compute_c_direct(hp, nfin)
            if cdir < best_c:
                best_c = cdir
                best_h = hp.copy()
    except Exception:
        pass

    best_h = project_feasible(best_h, len(best_h))
    best_c = compute_c_direct(best_h, len(best_h))
    return best_h, float(best_c), int(len(best_h))
```
